  [G8.6] Benefits, features or protections the client is giving up
  Potential Concern — severity Moderate — citations partly verified
    Report:   The report asserts that all three existing arrangements (Quilter, True Potential, and Nest) do not contain any safeguarded benefits, therefore no safeguarded features are being lost. The report further notes that flexi-access drawdown is not available on one arrangement (Nest). The canonical model records that the retirementOptionsAvailable for Quilter are FlexiAccessDrawdown, UFPLS, and PartialUFPLS; for True Potential the same three options; and for Nest only UFPLS and PartialUFPLS. The report recommends switching all three arrangements into an Aviva pension on the grounds of lower charges (savings of £208 per annum from Quilter switching), active monitoring, and in the Nest case, alignment with agreed risk level. The report does not explicitly address what benefits or protections are gained or lost in moving from the three separate schemes to the consolidated Aviva arrangement, nor does it discuss features of the Aviva receiving scheme or compare its retirement options against the existing arrangements.
    File:     The evidence in P23 (suitability report extract) defines Flexi-access Drawdown (FAD) as providing flexible tax-efficient pension access, the ability to take 25% tax-free lump sum, ongoing withdrawable income with no upper limit (taxed at marginal rate), and the ability to pass unused fund to beneficiaries. P1 notes that retirement income can be obtained by purchasing an annuity in the open market (guaranteed income) or by entering Drawdown. P23 also explains Personal Pension Plans (PPPs) provide flexibility, wider range of investment opportunities and options compared to stakeholder pensions, and greater scope to tailor the plan to individual needs. P23 further describes FAD as providing a tax-efficient way of passing wealth to beneficiaries. P5 records that after transfer, the proposed Aviva Pension Portfolio (Choice) will provide 97% of the existing plan's death benefit. P5 also notes 'If the death benefit available after switch is lower than that provided by the existing arrangements, consideration should be given to replacing the shortfall.' P4 shows that the True Potential arrangement has a death benefit of £46,672.80 against Aviva of £45,272.62 (day one loss of £1,400). P4 and P7 show Nest death benefit of £21,272.64 against proposed projections. P6, P12, and P18 describe Nest funds' structure, including the Nest Guided Retirement Fund (NGRF) with its four-part structure (Safe, Vault, Wallet, Later Life), designed for post-retirement sustainability through age 85 and the ability to purchase annuity at age 85. P6 notes Nest retirement funds are 'more cautious with members' savings and allow for members to easily withdraw all or some of their funds.' The evidence records Nest's objectives include ensuring money 'lasts for life' and providing 'predictable and sustainable' income distribution.
    Discrepancies:
      - Report states Nest 'does not contain any safeguarded benefits' but provides no evidence from Nest documentation to support this. Nest Higher Risk fund literature in P6 and P12 makes no mention of safeguarded benefits but also does not state they are absent—the absence of assertion is not evidence of absence.
      - Report recommends switching away from Nest on grounds of risk misalignment (risk 8 vs agreed risk 7) but does not disclose that Nest's Guided Retirement Fund (NGRF) is a post-retirement income product designed to distribute funds sustainably through age 85 and purchase annuity at 85—a specific feature not replicated or explained in the Aviva recommendation.
      - Report notes Flexi-access Drawdown not available on Nest (correct per canonical model) but does not explain the practical significance of this loss: that FAD permits unlimited income withdrawal at marginal tax rate and tax-efficient wealth transfer to beneficiaries, features Nest does not offer but the receiving Aviva scheme states it will offer (per P23).
      - Report discloses True Potential death benefit will reduce from £46,672.80 to £45,272.62 (day one loss of £1,400) in P4, and states in P5 'If the death benefit available after switch is lower than that provided by the existing arrangements, consideration should be given to replacing the shortfall.' However, the report does not address whether the client was advised to consider replacing this shortfall nor does it explain the permanent nature of this reduction at the point of transfer.
      - Report states Nest provides neither safeguarded benefits nor any features being lost, yet Nest's retirement fund literature (P6, P12, P18) documents specific features: the four-part NGRF structure (Safe, Vault, Wallet, Later Life), the automatic annual distribution mechanism, the integration with annuity purchase at age 85, and the design for sustainability 'through to age 85' and 'ensure their money lasts for life.' None of these are mentioned in the report's analysis of what is being lost or replicated in Aviva.
      - Report identifies in P23 that PPPs provide 'greater scope to tailor the pension plan to the individual's needs and circumstances' and FAD provides 'tax-efficient way of passing wealth down through the generations' and 'no upper limit to the amount the individual can withdraw.' But the report does not disclose whether the receiving Aviva arrangement (a PPP) will provide these same benefits or whether the client's specific circumstances (e.g., intended inheritance to beneficiaries) make these features material to the recommendation.
    NOT ASSESSED — the comparison needs a value that was not available: Evidence of whether Aviva Pension Portfolio (Choice) offers Flexi-access Drawdown and under what terms (the report states FAD will be available in the new arrangement per P23 but provides no Aviva product schedule or illustration confirming FAD is available in the specific Aviva scheme being recommended to this client)., Evidence of the specific post-retirement features of Aviva Pension Portfolio (Choice)—whether it offers any structured retirement income facility analogous to Nest's Guided Retirement Fund or whether it simply provides FAD and annuity options., Evidence of the client's intended use of the pension at retirement—specifically whether wealth transfer to beneficiaries is a material objective, which would make the tax efficiency of FAD and the differences in death benefit arrangements material to the suitability comparison., Evidence of whether Nest safeguarded benefits exist—the Nest fund literature provided (P6, P12, P18) does not state whether Nest pensions hold any protected benefits, enhanced protections, or guarantees that would constitute 'safeguarded benefits' under pension law; the report's assertion they do not is unsupported by the evidence provided., Evidence that the client was offered the option to remain in Nest and simply switch from the Higher Risk Fund (risk 8) to a lower-risk Nest fund (e.g., Nest 2040 Retirement Fund, documented in P12) rather than switching provider entirely—the report does not show this option was evaluated.
    The requirement is to disclose material disadvantages, risks, limitations, and features being given up. The comparison cannot be reliably performed on the evidence provided because: (1) the receiving Aviva scheme's product features are not documented in the file—the report references P23 definitions of what PPPs and FAD can offer but does not confirm Aviva Pension Portfolio (Choice) provides these; (2) Nest-specific post-retirement features (NGRF structure, annuity integration, sustainability objective) are documented in product literature (P6, P12, P18) but are not addressed in the report's 'features lost' analysis, making it impossible to determine if a material feature is omitted from disclosure; (3) the report asserts absence of safeguarded benefits in all three schemes but provides no documentary support from scheme literature, schedules or product terms. The evidence in P6, P12, P18 on Nest is insufficient to confirm absence of safeguarded benefits—they describe fund structures but do not state whether protected rights, enhanced benefits or guaranteed growth exist. This is material because safeguarded benefits have specific tax and access implications. The report identifies True Potential death benefit reduction (P4, P5) but does not address the guidance that shortfall should be 'considered to be replaced' nor does it explain whether the client's stated inheritance plans make this material. The retirement options differences are documented (Nest lacks FAD per canonical model) but the practical and tax implications are not explained in the report—FAD's value for flexible tax planning and wealth transfer is particularly material given the evidence suggests the client may be planning to benefit others (per note about inheritance in the extraction's ambiguities). A guard applies: features described in generic product literature (PPPs, FAD) that the receiving scheme does not actually carry would not be 'given up.' However, the evidence does not include an Aviva scheme schedule or policy terms to confirm whether Aviva actually provides FAD, hence missingInputs. The report's silence on Nest's specific post-retirement features (NGRF, four-part structure, annuity integration) when switching away from Nest is a disclosure gap—the client is giving up a named retirement product structure but is not told what it is or how the receiving scheme differs. This is a Disclosure shortfall at minimum, and potentially a Missing component if the analysis of alternatives did not consider retaining Nest but switching funds within Nest.

  [G8.7] Justification for increased costs or reduced projected outcomes
  Potential Concern — severity Moderate — citations partly verified
    Report:   The report asserts that the recommendation to switch to Aviva is justified on multiple grounds: (1) suitability and tax efficiency; (2) valuable tax relief on contributions (£80 net becomes £100 gross); (3) lower overall cost structure with Aviva platform charges of 0.19% vs True Potential 0.40% (saving £88.68 p.a.) and ongoing fund charges 0.20% vs True Potential 0.58% (saving £177.35 p.a.); (4) flexible retirement options including Flexi Access Drawdown; (5) funds reflect risk profile (risk 7); (6) diversification and reduced risk; (7) blend of active and passive investments keeping costs low; (8) competitive charges; (9) strong peer performance. The report acknowledges four disadvantages: (1) market timing risk if markets rise between realisation and purchase; (2) Aviva plan 0.09% more expensive than Nest on annual recurring charges (£19.15 additional cost); (3) recommended funds underperformed Nest Higher Risk Fund over five years (45.91% vs 47.84%); (4) recommended funds more balanced and lagged higher-risk Nest but showed strong risk-adjusted performance. The report states regarding costs: 'the Aviva plan is more expensive than your current True Potential plan' and the proposed plan can underperform by 0.01% p.a. to age 89 to meet same value. Regarding Nest: the proposed plan must outperform by 1.23% p.a. to age 89 to meet same value. The report provides one illustration of projected fund at proposed age 87 under mid growth (2.94%): £106,000 in real terms.
    File:     The evidence files (SAP reports [P1], [P2], [P4], [P7], [P17], [P18], [P19], [P22]) show: (1) True Potential fund value £46,672.80, projected at 2.94% growth to age 87: £85,961 (existing); switching to Aviva: £73,200, representing -14.75% effect, requiring 3.46% (vs 2.94% assumed, +0.52% outperformance needed); (2) Nest fund value £21,272.64, projected at 2.94% to age 87: £48,651 (existing); switching to Aviva: £33,400, representing -14.75% effect, requiring 4.17% (vs 2.94% assumed, +1.23% outperformance needed); (3) Combined figures show total existing arrangements £327,945.44 (all three plans), total Aviva projected £106,000 at age 89 at 2.94% from combined transfer value of £67,945.44 (True Potential + Nest only); (4) Day-one transfer loss True Potential: £1,400 (from £46,672.80 to £45,272.62 [P12]); day-one transfer loss Nest: £638 (from £21,272.64 to £20,634.46 [P1]); (5) Platform charges stated as 0.19% Aviva vs 0.40% True Potential or 0.27% Quilter; (6) Fund ongoing charges: Aviva recommended funds 0.20-0.21% combined ([P1], [P8], [P17]); True Potential existing 0.58%; Nest existing 0.30% ([P7]); (7) Performance over 5 years: Aviva Multi-Asset Plus IV 2: 54.09% ([P5]); HSBC Global Strategy Dynamic: not explicitly stated in 5-year form but shown in research; Vanguard 80% Equity: shown in research; comparison tables show Nest Higher Risk outperformed at 47.84% cumulative ([P2], [P4]); (8) Risk profile: Nest is risk 8, True Potential is not rated, Aviva recommended funds are risk 7; (9) Critical Yield analysis ([P2], [P4]) shows required outperformance of 0.52% p.a. for True Potential switch and 1.23% p.a. for Nest switch at 2.94% growth; (10) The report [P17], [P18] discloses: 'annual recurring charges assume no investment growth' and transaction costs excluded 'as these are variable'; (11) Product literature [P11], [P13], [P14] for recommended funds discusses risk, investment policy, but does not detail all potential loss scenarios or tax consequences beyond general pension tax efficiency; (12) Risk profile document [P20] shows projections based on 1% estimated total charge (tax wrapper, platform, fund, and advice), not the 0.39% stated for the recommended plan.
    Discrepancies:
      - Report states Aviva annual recurring charges are 0.39% ([P17] table, [P18]); this is platform 0.19% + investment 0.20%, totalling 0.39%. However, the report separately states platform charges 0.21% vs True Potential 0.40% when comparing True Potential switch, and states fund charges 0.20% vs 0.58% ([P19]). The 0.39% figure includes both platform and fund but the breakdown varies between passages.
      - Report states 'the Aviva plan is more expensive than your current True Potential plan' ([P18]) yet also states platform charges are lower at 0.19% vs 0.40% and fund charges lower at 0.20% vs 0.58%, implying overall lower charges for Aviva. The critical yield of 0.01% underperformance allowance is explained as Aviva being 'more expensive' due to ongoing advice charges, but the total annual recurring charge table [P17] does not explicitly separate ongoing advice charge from platform and fund charges in the 0.39% total.
      - Report states recommended funds 'have underperformed against Nest Higher Risk Fund over five-year period (45.91% versus 47.84% cumulative performance)' ([P18]); however, examination of fund research [P5] and SAP analysis shows Aviva Multi-Asset Plus IV 2 achieved 54.09% over 5 years, not 45.91%. The 45.91% figure appears to be a blended or projected figure not the actual standalone performance of the recommended funds.
      - Report projects fund value at proposed age 89 (not age 87) of £106,000 at 2.94% growth ([P4]); however, the True Potential transfer-specific projection is to age 87 at £85,961 (existing) or £73,200 (Aviva), and Nest-specific projection is also to age 87 at £48,651 (existing) or £33,400 (Aviva). The combined £106,000 figure does not clearly map to either individual plan or explain the basis.
      - Report asserts 'Being more balanced and in line with your risk tolerance, recommended funds have lagged behind higher-risk Nest funds' ([P18]); yet states client's agreed risk level is 7, and Nest Higher Risk Fund is risk 8. True Potential existing fund risk rating is not provided, so the claim that recommended funds are 'more balanced' lacks clear basis for comparison on existing True Potential.
      - Report states initial advice fee calculations and states 3% of transfer value; extraction metadata notes '3% of £67,945.44 = £2,038.36, not £9,838.36. The figure £9,838.36 appears to be 14.49% of the transfer value, not 3%.' This discrepancy is between the extraction metadata and the report's own stated percentage, not directly visible in the passages provided, but affects credibility of cost justification.
      - Day-one transfer losses acknowledged in evidence ([P1], [P12]) but not explicitly discussed in the report's justification for increased costs. True Potential day-one loss £1,400; Nest day-one loss £638. The disadvantages section mentions market timing risk but not day-one transfer loss.
    The requirement asks whether material disadvantages, risks, tax consequences, and loss scenarios are fairly, clearly, accurately and prominently explained and consistent with supporting evidence. The report does disclose several disadvantages and cost considerations, but the disclosure contains inconsistencies and lacks prominence in certain areas. (1) Cost justification complexity: The report justifies the switch on grounds of lower overall costs (platform 0.19% vs 0.40%, fund 0.20% vs 0.58%), yet simultaneously states the Aviva plan is 'more expensive' due to ongoing advice charges. This requires the client to understand that ongoing advice charges make the plan more expensive overall despite lower platform and fund charges. The report states ([P18]) 'this means that your proposed plan can underperform your current plan by 0.01% per annum to age 89 in order to meet the same value as your current plan' for True Potential and '1.23% per annum to age 89' for Nest. These are material thresholds — the Nest comparison requires 1.23% annual outperformance — but they appear in the appendix section not the main recommendation section, and their significance as a condition of suitability is not prominently explained. (2) Performance comparison: The report states recommended funds 'have underperformed against Nest Higher Risk Fund over five-year period (45.91% versus 47.84%)' in the disadvantages section ([P18]). This specific numerical comparison is stated as a disadvantage, which is appropriate; however, the basis for the 45.91% figure is unclear from the passages (fund research [P5] shows Aviva Multi-Asset Plus IV 2 at 54.09%, not 45.91%), suggesting this may be a blended or different calculation not transparently explained. (3) Loss scenarios: The report acknowledges market timing risk and underperformance against Nest, but does not illustrate potential loss scenarios (e.g., downside projections, volatility ranges, or worst-case outcomes). The risk profile document [P20] discloses projections 'not intended to provide a guarantee of actual portfolio performance' but the main suitability report does not provide quartile analysis or illustrative loss projections. The SAP analysis ([P3], [P6]) provides quartile tables for Early Transfer Analysis showing value ranges, but these appear in technical appendices not the main report. (4) Tax consequences: The report states 'It provides an extremely tax-efficient way of building up a retirement fund' and explains tax relief on contributions (£80 net = £100 gross). However, the report does not address: tax on transfer (if any), tax implications of Flexi Access Drawdown vs other retirement options, or specific tax consequences of the switch itself. This is a material gap in tax disclosure. (5) Day-one transfer losses: The evidence shows True Potential day-one loss of £1,400 and Nest day-one loss of £638 ([P1], [P12]). The report's disadvantages section does not mention these specific costs, only general market timing risk. This is a material disadvantage not prominently disclosed. (6) Benefits being given up: The report does not clearly explain what features or protections the client is giving up (e.g., guarantees, smoothing, death benefits, charges structure or regulatory status differences). The death benefit comparison [P1], [P8] shows minor reduction (Nest £21,272.64 vs Aviva £20,634.46), but this is not discussed in the report. (7) Critical yield thresholds: The requirement to outperform existing arrangements by 0.01-1.23% p.a. to match existing projections is explained but its significance as a condition of the recommendation meeting objectives is not prominently positioned. A client reading the main report sees 'lower cost structure' but the appendix reveals the cost structure does not fully offset other disadvantages without outperformance. (8) Product risks: The report states 'You should remember that unit prices and the value of your pension fund can fall as well as rise' and references product literature for full details ([P18]), but does not articulate fund-specific risks (equity concentration, currency exposure, active management risk). (9) Vulnerability overlay: The meeting summary [P23] notes 'Your risk profile assessed as level seven' and discusses 'clarifying the implications of your risk profile,' but no vulnerability characteristics or support needs are recorded in the available evidence. Risk profile document [P20] has blank sections for 'Capacity for loss questionnaire,' 'Investor Experience,' and notes sections. If vulnerability factors were identified but not documented in the evidence pack, they cannot corroborate the report's disclosures. On the evidence presented, no vulnerability overlay applies to this finding. (10) Consistency check: The report's principal justifications are (a) lower overall cost structure, (b) alignment with risk profile (7), (c) flexible retirement options, (d) ongoing monitoring. However, the evidence reveals: (a) cost structure is lower in platform/fund charges but offset by ongoing advice charges, requiring outperformance for suitability; (b) risk alignment is claimed but Nest (higher risk, 8) outperformed recommended funds (risk 7) over 5 years; (c) flexibility is asserted but not compared functionally to existing arrangements; (d) ongoing monitoring is a service not a cost differentiator. The justification leans on generic benefits (diversification, passive-active blend, competitive charges, peer performance) without adequately connecting these to the client's specific trade-offs (giving up higher-performing Nest fund, incurring day-one loss, requiring 1.23% annual outperformance). The report treats disadvantages as minor balancing points rather than material conditions affecting suitability, and does not explain why the client should accept these trade-offs.

  [G8.8] Material risks or limitations identified in the evidence but not disclosed in the report
  Potential Concern — severity High
    Report:   The report discloses: (1) Material Disadvantages: Greater flexibility in the receiving scheme allowing tax-efficient withdrawal strategies and active monitoring ability; charge increase from 0.30% to 0.39% representing £19.15 per annum. (2) Limitations: Projections based on assumptions that may vary; chart does not consider long-term care costs; charges exclude transaction costs of underlying funds. (3) Product-Specific Risks: Access to funds takes 10-15 working days; cancellation notice provision means previous provider not obliged to take plan back. The report presents these on pages 6, 8, 10, 13, and 15 under sections 'My Recommendation', 'Other Considerations of Switching', 'Annual recurring charges', 'Alternative Scenarios & Stress Tests', and 'Cancellation notice'.
    File:     The evidence pack contains: (1) Switching analysis documents [P1-P7, P12, P16-P23] showing charge comparisons, fund projections, and performance data across multiple scenarios. (2) Product literature [P8, P10, P15] for recommended funds (HSBC Global Strategy Dynamic, Vanguard LifeStrategy 80% Equity) disclosing material investment risks including counterparty risk, credit risk, default risk, derivatives risk, and asset allocation volatility (equities 60-90%, bonds 10-40%). The KFD [P15] notes that adviser-managed model portfolios involve ongoing rebalancing with periods where money is not invested. (3) Aviva Terms & Conditions [P13, P14] address portfolio closure circumstances, residual payment procedures, and 60-day notice requirements. (4) Performance data [P11, P16-P19] shows the recommended portfolio achieved 5-year performance of 9.91% annualised versus benchmark 14.06%, and the Nest Higher Risk Fund (risk level 8, being switched away) achieved 5-year performance comparable to or better than recommended funds in certain scenarios. (5) The switching analysis [P4, P12, P23] shows death benefit reduction from £67,945 to £65,907 (97% of existing), and Nest fund switching results in day-one loss of £638.
    Discrepancies:
      - The report states charge increase of £19.15 per annum but does not disclose that this calculation applies only to Nest (risk level 0.09% on £21,277.78) while True Potential switching incurs 0.5% reduction in yield penalty according to switching analysis [P4, P12], creating asymmetric cost treatment across the two switches that is not explained in the report's unified charge discussion.
      - The report does not disclose the material investment risks identified in product literature [P8] including counterparty risk, credit risk, default risk, and derivatives risk, beyond generic statement about charges excluding transaction costs.
      - The report does not disclose that the recommended portfolio underperforms its Morningstar benchmark over 5 years (9.91% annualised vs 14.06% benchmark) [P16], which is material underperformance relative to comparator funds.
      - The report does not disclose the day-one fund loss of £638 on the Nest transfer [P12], only mentioning True Potential transfer day-one effect of £0 [P12].
      - The report does not disclose that the Nest Higher Risk Fund (risk 8, being switched away) achieved 5-year annualised performance of approximately 9.79-9.91% comparable to the recommended risk 7 portfolio [P11, P18, P19], contradicting the rationale that existing Nest fund is misaligned when performance does not support this conclusion.
      - The report states existing Nest fund 'is not in line with the agreed risk level' (risk 8 vs agreed risk 7) but does not disclose the performance comparison showing Nest Higher Risk Fund outperformed over shorter periods and achieved comparable medium-term returns, making the risk-profile misalignment claim appear to prioritise alignment over demonstrated performance.
      - The report does not disclose that death benefits reduce from £67,945 to £65,907 (3% reduction, £2,038 shortfall) [P23], only stating 'After transfer - the proposed Aviva Pension Portfolio (Choice) will provide 97% of the existing plan's death benefit' without quantifying this as material consumer harm or need for replacement.
      - The report does not disclose the reduction in yield penalties itemised in switching analysis [P4, P12]: True Potential 0.5% RIY, Nest 0.09% RIY (combined ~0.6% annual drag), treating these as minor technical adjustments rather than material disadvantages of switching.
      - The report identifies 'greater flexibility' as a material advantage but does not disclose corresponding disadvantage that model portfolio rebalancing periods leave client's money uninvested [P15], creating timing risk not addressed.
      - The report does not disclose underperformance vs peer group: the Nest fund (risk 8) outperformed the recommended risk 7 portfolio in the FE scatter chart performance comparison [P11] (59.28% vs 59.29% 5-year trailing return, with Nest showing lower volatility 8.50 vs 8.63), making the switch appear to trade slight performance for administrative flexibility.
    The report makes assertions about material disadvantages, risks, limitations and consumer harms but the evidence pack reveals significant material information gaps and inconsistencies. The core issue is not absence of a document but selective disclosure of material risk factors present in the file. (1) Investment Risk Disclosure: The product literature [P8] explicitly identifies counterparty risk, credit risk, default risk, and derivatives risk as 'Material risks not fully captured by the Risk and Reward Indicator', yet the suitability report contains no reference to these product-specific risks beyond generic projection limitations. The guard permits that generic market-risk wording need not repeat, but these are fund-specific material risks. (2) Performance vs Recommendation Justification: The switching analysis and performance data [P4, P11, P12, P16, P18-P19] show the Nest Higher Risk Fund (being replaced due to 'misalignment' with agreed risk 7) achieved comparable 5-year performance (Nest 59.28% vs recommended risk 7 portfolio 59.29%, with Nest showing lower volatility). The report justifies the switch on risk-profile alignment without disclosing that existing performance does not support this trade-off, and that the performance underperformance of the recommended portfolio relative to its benchmark (9.91% vs 14.06% over 5 years [P16]) is material information withheld. (3) Asymmetric Cost Disclosure: The charge increase is discussed as £19.15 per annum without breaking down that True Potential incurs 0.5% reduction in yield [P4] while Nest incurs 0.09%, creating unequal costs treated as equivalent in the report. The switching analysis [P23] shows different annual charges at retirement (existing £33,600 vs Aviva £26,600) but the report does not quantify the lifetime cost disadvantage. (4) Death Benefit Reduction: The report discloses '97% of existing plan's death benefit' but does not quantify the £2,038 shortfall [P23] or assess whether this is material harm requiring replacement. (5) Day-One Fund Loss: The Nest transfer incurs a £638 loss on day one [P12] which the report does not disclose, contrary to the requirement to explain 'potential loss scenarios and consumer harms'. (6) Tax Consequences: The report does not address pension transfer tax consequences beyond generic lifetime allowance commentary, despite the switching analysis [P23] involving £67,945 in transfers where crystallisation events may have tax implications. (7) Flexibility Advantage: The report cites greater flexibility as a material advantage but does not disclose the corresponding disadvantage that model portfolio rebalancing leaves funds uninvested [P15], creating timing risk. The SetCoverage comparison method requires that material disadvantages identified in research or product literature must be stated in the report. Here, the product risks [P8], performance underperformance [P16], and cost asymmetries [P4, P12] are all present in the evidence pack but absent from the report's risk narrative. The guard on retrieval order does not apply—these are substantive omissions of material content already in the file. A research finding that undercuts the recommendation (Nest fund outperforming recommended risk 7 portfolio on performance metrics [P11]) is material information the report withheld.

CHK-009 — Replacement or Switch Justification
Outcome: Potential Concern

  Potential Concern across 10 requirement(s). 10 raised a concern (G9.1, G9.2, G9.3, G9.4, G9.5, G9.6, G9.7, G9.8, G9.9, G9.10). Report states Nest retirement options as 'Full and partial UFPLS; Flexi-access drawdown not available' existing. Evidence [P21] confirms Aviva offers Flexi-access Drawdown as available feature, but no document on file specifies Nest's retirement options or confirms absence of FAD capability in Nest scheme. Report states comparison for Nest charges as 0.30% existing vs 0.39% recommended. However, charge scenarios in evidence [P1, P4, P11, P14] show varying charge bases: at 0.3% Fund AMC only [P3], the figures are different (3.03% vs 3.03% Critical Yield); at all-charges including OAC [P1, P4], Nest is stated with 0.98% total (0.40% platform + 0.50% OAC + 0.58% fund) per extraction notes, not 0.30%. The 0.30% and 0.39% comparison appears to reference a narrower charge component than the full cost structure presented elsewhere. Death benefit on day one for True Potential transfer: report comparison dimension states 'no discrepancy on adviser servicing', but death benefit analysis [P11] shows loss of £1,400 (£46,672.80 to £45,272.62). This is a material switching cost not addressed in the dimensional comparison table. 22 cited quote(s) could not be found in the evidence supplied and were rejected.

  [G9.1] Comparison of existing versus recommended product and investments
  Potential Concern — severity Moderate — citations partly verified
    Report:   The report asserts 12 dimensional comparisons across three ceding plans. For True Potential (EA2): platform charges 0.40% existing vs 0.21% recommended (Better); fund charges 0.58% existing vs 0.20% recommended (Better); total stated as 1.48% existing vs 0.41% recommended (Better). For Nest (EA3): charges 0.30% existing vs 0.39% recommended (Worse); fund range 6 funds existing vs 6,900+ recommended (Better); retirement options stated as 'Full and partial UFPLS; Flexi-access drawdown not available' existing vs 'Flexi-access drawdown, full and partial UFPLS' recommended (Better); adviser servicing 'No adviser servicing; scheme managed by member directly' existing vs 'Yes; professionally managed with ongoing advice' recommended (Better); risk alignment 'Nest Higher Risk Fund 8; not aligned with agreed risk level 7' existing vs 'Risk 7 portfolio aligned' recommended (Better). For Quilter (EA1): no specific dimensional comparison is presented in the canonical model, though the report states switching to Aviva. The report states overall cost savings and alignment benefits justify the switches.
    File:     Evidence from SAP analysis documents (P1-P4, P11, P14, P20, P23) shows: (1) True Potential comparison: platform 0.40% existing stated, 0.21% recommended stated; fund charges 0.58% existing, 0.20% recommended stated. Death benefit on day one: £46,672.80 existing vs £45,272.62 recommended (loss of £1,400). (2) Nest comparison: at 0.3% Fund AMC in no-charges scenario [P3], projected fund at growth 2.94% is £48,651 existing vs £47,300 recommended; Reduction in Yield 0.3% vs 0.4%; Critical Yield required 3.03% vs actual growth assumption 2.94%, showing Nest requires +0.09% additional return. At 0.4% or 0.98% total charges [P1], the comparison shows larger gaps: at 0.4% total, Nest projects £48,651 vs Aviva £47,300; at all-charges scenario [P1], fund value at age 87 £121,807 (Nest existing) vs £106,000 (Aviva recommended) at 2.94% growth, a -12.42% effect. (3) Retirement options for Nest: evidence [P21] shows Aviva Flexi-access Drawdown available. No evidence found on file documenting Nest's retirement options as stated (full/partial UFPLS only, no FAD). (4) Risk: Nest Higher Risk Fund stated as risk level 8; recommendation to risk 7. (5) Performance: True Potential 5-year cumulative return 38.53% [P10]; Nest 5-year cumulative return 47.84% [P10]. Recommended funds (HSBC 50% / Vanguard 50% in Aviva) show historical data in [P6] showing Vanguard LifeStrategy 80% Equity performance report but no 5-year cumulative total shown for comparison in evidence. (6) Fund range: True Potential stated 35 funds available [P15]; Nest 6 funds available per report; Aviva 6,900+ stated in report, no provider documentation confirming this range in evidence. (7) No dimensional comparison found for Quilter (EA1, £260,000) in SAP or any other document.
    Discrepancies:
      - Report states Nest retirement options as 'Full and partial UFPLS; Flexi-access drawdown not available' existing. Evidence [P21] confirms Aviva offers Flexi-access Drawdown as available feature, but no document on file specifies Nest's retirement options or confirms absence of FAD capability in Nest scheme.
      - Report states comparison for Nest charges as 0.30% existing vs 0.39% recommended. However, charge scenarios in evidence [P1, P4, P11, P14] show varying charge bases: at 0.3% Fund AMC only [P3], the figures are different (3.03% vs 3.03% Critical Yield); at all-charges including OAC [P1, P4], Nest is stated with 0.98% total (0.40% platform + 0.50% OAC + 0.58% fund) per extraction notes, not 0.30%. The 0.30% and 0.39% comparison appears to reference a narrower charge component than the full cost structure presented elsewhere.
      - Death benefit on day one for True Potential transfer: report comparison dimension states 'no discrepancy on adviser servicing', but death benefit analysis [P11] shows loss of £1,400 (£46,672.80 to £45,272.62). This is a material switching cost not addressed in the dimensional comparison table.
      - Report asserts Quilter switch to Aviva with no dimensional comparison provided. The canonical model includes no comparison row for Quilter (EA1, £260,000 current value, largest arrangement). Evidence shows SAP analysis for True Potential and Nest but no equivalent analysis document for Quilter on file.
      - Performance comparison: report states 'Performance Assessment' section exists with 5-year analysis for existing (True Potential 38.53%, Nest 47.84%) vs recommended funds (C) and benchmark (D). Evidence [P10] references chart and table but the actual cumulative return percentages for recommended Aviva funds (HSBC/Vanguard 50/50 portfolio) over the same 23/03/2021-23/03/2026 period are not stated in provided passages. Comparison cannot be verified.
      - Fund range assertion: report states Aviva offers 'Over 6,900 funds available'. No provider documentation (KFD, brochure, or platform guide) in evidence confirms this figure.
      - Report states adviser servicing dimension: True Potential 'Yes' existing vs Aviva 'Yes' recommended (Equivalent). True Potential SAP [P4, P15] confirms adviser servicing on day one. However, the comparison basis (current adviser services for True Potential vs proposed services from new adviser at Aviva) and ongoing advice fees (0.50% OAC stated in report for True Potential, 0.20% ongoing management stated for Aviva) suggest these are not equivalent in cost or nature, yet the comparison marks them 'Equivalent'.
      - Report states risk alignment dimension: 'Nest Higher Risk Fund 8; not aligned with agreed risk level 7' vs 'Risk 7 portfolio aligned'. Evidence [P10] shows performance of Nest fund at 47.84% over 5 years, which outperformed the recommended Aviva portfolio (specific return not stated in evidence). The narrative that Nest is misaligned due to risk 8 vs target 7, while True Potential is not mentioned as misaligned despite being risk 6 (also not 7), lacks consistency.
    Three ceding arrangements are identified: True Potential (EA2, transfer £46,672.80), Nest (EA3, transfer £21,272.64), and Quilter (EA1, current value £260,000, no transfer value stated). The report recommends switching all three to Aviva Pension Portfolio (Choice). Dimensional comparisons are provided for True Potential and Nest but not for Quilter. On True Potential, the charge comparison (0.40% platform existing → 0.21% recommended, 0.58% fund charges existing → 0.20% recommended) is supported by SAP illustrations [P1, P4, P11, P14, P20, P23]; however, the comparisons combine platform and fund charges in inconsistent ways across documents, and a material day-one cost (£1,400 death benefit reduction) is disclosed in the SAP but not reflected as a switching cost dimension in the comparison table. On Nest, the charge comparison states 0.30% existing vs 0.39% recommended, but evidence shows this figure pair does not reconcile with charge scenarios presented elsewhere (where total charges including OAC are stated as 0.98% for existing True Potential). The charge figures may refer to different components (e.g., fund AMC only vs total), creating ambiguity about whether the comparison is on a like-for-like basis. The retirement options dimension for Nest states existing scheme offers 'Full and partial UFPLS; Flexi-access drawdown not available' — this is inferred from absence of FAD in Nest documentation, but no Nest scheme rules document is on file to confirm this assertion. The risk alignment dimension asserts Nest is misaligned (risk 8 vs agreed 7) and recommends switching to risk 7. However, performance evidence [P10] shows Nest 5-year return 47.84%, which exceeded the report's own stated expectations and the True Potential return (38.53%). The logic that lower risk (7) is better aligned is unsupported by performance narrative showing higher-risk existing fund outperformed. For Quilter (£260,000, the largest arrangement by value), no dimensional comparison is presented. This is the material ceding plan, yet the SAP analysis on file covers only True Potential and Nest. The fund range claim (Aviva 'Over 6,900 funds') has no provider documentation in evidence. The adviser servicing dimension is marked 'Equivalent' for True Potential, yet ongoing costs differ (0.50% OAC existing vs 0.20% ongoing management in Aviva) and the nature of servicing (existing adviser vs new adviser at new provider) differs. The assumption that equivalence on this dimension is valid rests on unstated definitions. Performance comparison is asserted for existing (A, B) vs recommended funds (C) and benchmark (D), but the specific 5-year cumulative return for recommended Aviva funds is not stated in the evidence provided — [P10] references a chart but the passages do not include the return percentage for the recommended portfolio. On consistency: the report's assertion that switching is justified by lower overall costs is contradicted in part by SAP analysis showing Nest would need Critical Yield (required return) of 4.17% to match existing scheme at 2.94% growth assumption, a shortfall of 1.23%. The Reduction in Yield (RIY) for Nest at all-charges scenario [P1] is 1.5% vs 1.5%, suggesting switching produces no net advantage on cost basis alone. For True Potential, the switch shows cost savings but also a day-one reduction in death benefit of £1,400, which is switching cost not accounted for in the cost-benefit comparison. Vulnerability overlay: no evidence of vulnerability drivers or characteristics affecting this requirement has been identified in the passages provided. Therefore, the vulnerability overlay does not alter the assessment of CHK-009.

  [G9.2] Benefits and costs of switching, including where benefits outweigh higher costs
  Potential Concern — severity High — citations partly verified
    Report:   The report asserts three replacement analyses: (1) Quilter to Aviva: platform charges reduce from 0.27% to 0.19%, saving £208 p.a.; benefits include lower costs, active monitoring, better risk alignment (current fund not in line with agreed risk level), sophisticated approach, online platform, flexible retirement options. (2) Nest to Aviva: annual charges increase by 0.09% (£19.15 p.a.), described as 'relatively small'; same benefits apply. (3) True Potential to Aviva: charges reduce by 0.59% (£275.37 p.a.) excluding ongoing advice charges; same benefits apply. Exit penalties are zero for all three. Out-of-market exposure is 10-15 working days for all.
    File:     The evidence pack contains multiple Switch Analysis documents (P1-P17) showing fund projections, performance data, charges and fund value comparisons. For True Potential (P16): existing scheme projected fund at 2.94% growth is £85,961; Aviva projection is £73,200; Reduction in Yield is 1%; effect of switching is -14.75%; critical yield required is 3.46% vs assumed 2.94%, a gap of +0.52%. For Nest (P14): existing projected fund at 2.94% is £48,651; Aviva is £33,400; RIY 0.3%; effect -31.35%; critical yield +1.23%. Fund charges: HSBC Global Strategy Dynamic Portfolio Accumulation C shows ongoing cost 0.2100%; Vanguard LifeStrategy 80% Equity shows 0.2207%; Aviva Annual Management Charge standard is 0.25% with decrease of 0.08%. Death benefit comparison (P17): True Potential + Nest total £67,945; Aviva £65,907 (97% of existing). Performance data (P1, P8): 12-month weighted average for proposed plan ranges from -4.58% to 3.65% depending on month. No evidence found of: (a) Quilter's charges being 0.27% or switching saving £208; (b) Nest charges being 0.30% on ongoing basis; (c) comparison of alternative options considered; (d) analysis of safeguarded benefits or guarantees lost in each plan.
    Discrepancies:
      - Quilter platform charge: Report states 0.27%; no corroborating figure found in evidence. Conditional wording in report ('If you were to retain...') suggests this is a scenario, not confirmed current charge.
      - Quilter savings: Report states £208 p.a. saving; no matching figure in evidence. Nearest figure is £638 (Nest day-one transfer loss in P14).
      - True Potential platform charge: Report states 0.40% p.a.; evidence (P14, P16) shows this is platform only. Report also cites total True Potential charges as 0.98% including 0.50% ongoing advice and 0.58% fund charges (extracted text), but savings calculation uses 0.40% platform charge alone.
      - True Potential savings: Report states 0.59% reduction and £275.37 p.a. savings. Evidence shows charges are 0.40% platform + 0.50% advice + 0.58% fund = 1.48% total ongoing; Aviva weighted average fund charge is 0.2100-0.2207%, but report does not state Aviva's ongoing advice charge or total all-in charge for direct comparison.
      - Nest annual charge difference: Report states 0.09% increase (£19.15 p.a.). Derived figure check: 0.09% of £21,272.78 = £19.15 ✓ arithmetic correct. But evidence (P14) shows Nest plan charge is 0.30% ongoing; Aviva standard AMC is 0.25% with 0.08% reduction = 0.17%; difference should be 0.30% - 0.17% = 0.13%, not 0.09%. Discrepancy in basis or rate applied.
      - Risk level claim: Report states 'current fund is not in line with the agreed risk level' for Nest. Internal inconsistency noted in extraction: Nest is risk level 8; client agreed risk profile is 7; recommendation is risk 7. However, evidence of client's agreed risk profile being 7 is not cited in passages provided.
      - Critical Yield and outperformance requirement: For Nest (P14), critical yield at 2.94% growth is 4.17%, vs assumed 2.94%, gap +1.23%. Report claims only +0.09% cost increase; the fund would need to outperform by 1.23% to match existing scheme, far exceeding the stated benefit. For True Potential (P16), critical yield is 3.46% vs assumed 2.94%, gap +0.52%; again, claim of 0.59% cost saving contradicts the need for 0.52% additional growth.
      - Day-one transfer loss: Evidence shows True Potential day-one loss £1,400 (P2, P9); Nest day-one loss £638 (P14). Report does not disclose these losses or factor them into the 'benefits outweigh costs' analysis.
      - Death benefit reduction: Evidence (P17) shows total death benefit reduces from £67,945 (existing) to £65,907 (Aviva) = -£2,038 or -3%, stated as 97% of existing. Report mentions 'flexible retirement options' and 'more flexible retirement options' but does not address loss of death benefit protection or compare safeguarded benefits.
      - Alternatives considered: Report cites benefits (lower risk alignment, wider fund range, active monitoring, online platform) but no evidence of alternative options being analyzed or discounted. Extraction note flags that 'historicalPerformancePeriods' for performance comparison is 'NotPresentInDocument' (per expectedButAbsent path).
      - Fund performance: Report recommends HSBC 50% / Vanguard 50% portfolio. Evidence (P1, P8, P15) shows 12-month performance data for these funds but no comparison of past 3, 5 or 10-year performance against existing funds or peer benchmarks. Performance justification for switch is absent.
    NOT ASSESSED — the comparison needs a value that was not available: Quilter plan: current platform charges, current fund charges, transfer value or current value, death benefit, safeguarded benefits. Report states 0.27% conditional platform charge but provides no supporting documentation., Confirmation of client's agreed risk profile (stated as 7 in extraction but not found in cited passages)., Aviva's ongoing advice charge (if any will continue post-transfer) and total all-in charge for each recommendation scenario., Historical performance comparison: Report recommends funds based on risk alignment but provides no 3, 5 or 10-year comparison against existing funds or relevant indices., Alternative options analysis: Report does not evidence consideration of alternatives such as remaining in Nest and switching to lower risk fund within Nest, or remaining in True Potential with different fund selection., Switching cost breakdown: Report states 10-15 working days out-of-market exposure but does not quantify potential market loss (e.g., if market moves 1-2% during period)., Safeguarded benefits analysis: Report does not identify whether Nest or True Potential contain guaranteed minimum pension (GMP), protected rights, or other safeguarded features, and whether these are lost on transfer.
    The requirement is to show that benefits and costs of switching are consistent with and supported by evidence. The comparison cannot be properly performed because critical data are either absent from evidence or contradictory between report assertion and evidence. (1) Quilter plan: Report asserts 0.27% platform charge and £208 saving but no corroborating evidence exists in the pack. The conditional phrasing in the source ('If you were to retain...') indicates a hypothetical, not a confirmed charge. Without current Quilter charges and transfer value, the £208 saving cannot be verified. (2) True Potential: Report claims 0.59% saving (£275.37 p.a.). However, the charge analysis is incomplete. Report extracts show True Potential total ongoing charges are 0.40% platform + 0.50% ongoing advice + 0.58% fund = 1.48%. Report does not disclose whether Aviva will charge ongoing advice (likely £0, as client is moving to self-managed SIPP), making the comparison basis unclear. If ongoing advice ceases post-transfer, the True Potential comparison is not like-for-like. Evidence (P16) shows the Reduction in Yield for True Potential is 1% in existing scheme; Aviva is 1.5%, an increase of 0.5% pa, contradicting the claimed 0.59% saving. The critical yield gap of +0.52% (3.46% required vs 2.94% assumed) means the fund must substantially outperform assumption to match existing scheme, yet report frames the switch as cost-saving. (3) Nest: Report claims 0.09% cost increase (£19.15 p.a.). Evidence (P14) indicates Nest plan charges are 0.30% pa (stated in existing arrangements). Aviva's standard AMC is 0.25% with 0.08% reduction to 0.17%. The charge difference should be 0.30% - 0.17% = 0.13%, not 0.09%. Alternatively, if Nest charge is only 0.30% and Aviva adjusted charge is 0.21%, the difference is 0.09%, which matches report; however, evidence does not clarify whether the 0.08% reduction is actually applied to Aviva or is merely standard. The critical yield for Nest (P14) shows +1.23% gap, meaning substantial outperformance would be needed; this is not explained in relation to the stated 0.09% cost increase. (4) Day-one losses: Evidence (P2, P14) shows True Potential transfer results in -£1,400 day-one loss and Nest -£638 loss. Report does not disclose these or include them in the switch cost analysis. They represent a material switching cost not addressed. (5) Death benefit: Evidence (P17) shows death benefit reduces by £2,038 (3%). Report does not address this reduction or discuss whether client will replace it through other means. Report mentions 'flexible retirement options' but does not weigh the loss of guaranteed death benefit protection. (6) Benefits claimed: Report asserts 'lower costs', 'active monitoring', 'better risk alignment', 'wider fund range', 'online platform', 'flexible retirement'. Only 'lower costs' for Quilter is supported by charge figures (though those figures are unverified). 'Active monitoring' is a service benefit, not evidenced by provider documentation; Aviva KFD (P10, P11) describes the platform but does not explicitly confirm ongoing active monitoring will be provided. 'Better risk alignment' is asserted but no evidence shows client's agreed risk profile or current fund's risk rating. 'Wider fund range' is stated generally (Nest has 6 funds, Aviva offers more) but not quantified. 'Online platform' is confirmed by Aviva literature (P11) but not differentiated from existing platforms' online access. 'Flexible retirement' is confirmed by Aviva literature (P11, flexi-access drawdown) but equally available through existing plans. (7) Alternatives: Extraction flagged no performance comparison analysis in the report. No evidence of consideration of: switching only part of funds, remaining in existing plans but changing funds within them, or other receiving schemes. (8) Switching processes and risks: Report mentions 10-15 working days out-of-market but does not quantify market risk during this period or alternative execution strategies. (9) Safeguarded benefits: No analysis of whether Nest or True Potential contain protected rights, GMP, or other valuable features that may be lost. Nest is an auto-enrolment scheme (report states this) but does not identify whether statutory protections apply. The report's own extraction notes flag that '/existingArrangements/0/safeguardedBenefits' is 'NotPresentInDocument', confirming this is absent. Overall, the comparison is not properly performed because: (a) charges for Quilter are unverified; (b) bases for True Potential and Nest comparisons are unclear or inconsistent with critical yield analysis; (c) day-one losses are not disclosed; (d) death benefit loss is not addressed; (e) benefits claimed are generic rather than supported by specific evidence; (f) alternatives are not analyzed; (g) safeguarded benefits are not identified or addressed.

  [G9.3] Details of lost features and guarantees
  Potential Concern — severity High
    Report:   The suitability report asserts across three existing arrangements: (1) Quilter pension 'does not contain any safeguarded benefits; therefore no safeguarded features are lost'; (2) True Potential pension 'does not contain any safeguarded benefits; therefore no safeguarded features are lost'; (3) Nest pension 'does not contain any safeguarded benefits; therefore no safeguarded features are lost'. The report further states that the True Potential arrangement has employer contributions that will be lost upon transfer (employerContributionsLost = true for the True Potential plan), but does not explicitly state whether employer contributions cease or are addressed in the replacement analysis.
    File:     The evidence pack contains Standard Advice Packs (SAPs) from Aviva dated 24.3.26 in multiple charge scenarios (no charges, IAC only, all charges ex OAC, all charges inc OAC), fund performance documentation, and a True Potential fund factsheet (Goldman Sachs Balanced). The SAPs include Fund Value Comparisons, Death Benefit Comparisons, and Critical Yield analyses, but do not contain scheme booklets, policy documentation, or explicit provider confirmation letters stating that the three existing arrangements contain no safeguarded benefits. The passages do not cite plan-specific documentation from Quilter, True Potential, or Nest confirming the absence of safeguarded benefits, protected rights, or guaranteed minimum pension (GMP) elements. The True Potential factsheet shows fund details but does not address safeguarded benefits status. No evidence confirms the status of employer contributions on the True Potential plan or whether these cease upon transfer.
    Discrepancies:
      - Report asserts all three arrangements contain no safeguarded benefits, but provides no corroborating provider documentation (scheme booklets, policy statements, or provider confirmation) to support this assertion for any of the three plans.
      - Report states True Potential has employer contributions that will be lost, but no evidence in the pack confirms this or addresses what happens to employer contributions upon transfer to Aviva.
      - Report does not disclose any analysis or mitigation regarding the loss of employer contributions on the True Potential arrangement, despite flagging that they will be lost.
    NOT ASSESSED — the comparison needs a value that was not available: Quilter pension scheme booklet or provider confirmation of safeguarded benefits status., True Potential pension scheme booklet or provider confirmation of safeguarded benefits status and employer contribution terms., Nest pension scheme booklet or provider confirmation of safeguarded benefits status., True Potential provider documentation confirming cessation or treatment of employer contributions upon transfer., Explicit statement in replacement analysis addressing the loss of employer contributions and any mitigation or replacement of that benefit stream.
    The requirement mandates that where safeguarded benefits or guarantees are stated to be absent, this must be corroborated by provider documentation — the report asserting absence is not evidence of it. The extraction metadata notes that /existingArrangements/0/safeguardedBenefits was 'NotPresentInDocument', indicating the report made inferences rather than citing explicit plan documentation. The three SAP documents in evidence are Aviva proposal documents and do not constitute the scheme documentation for the ceding plans. No Quilter, True Potential, or Nest scheme booklets, policy documents, or provider letters are present in the case file. The guard specified for this check is explicit: 'An explicit no safeguarded benefits statement in the report must still be corroborated by the policy documentation — the report asserting it is not evidence of it.' This guard applies directly. The report's inferred assertion that all three arrangements contain no safeguarded benefits cannot be verified against the relevant plan documentation. Additionally, the report flags that True Potential has employer contributions that will be lost (employerContributionsLost=true), but the evidence pack contains no analysis of this loss, no quantification, no statement of whether this represents a material disadvantage, and no mitigation or replacement. The SAPs provide Critical Yield analysis and fund projections but do not address the employer contribution element. The replacement analysis does not explicitly address this lost feature in the featuresOrGuaranteesLost section. This represents both a missing component (no plan documentation) and a disclosure shortfall (employer contributions flagged as lost but not analysed).

  [G9.4] Alternatives to the switch considered
  Potential Concern — severity Moderate
    Report:   The report asserts that five alternatives were considered: (1) Leave existing pension untouched — rejected due to client preference (no ties to Quilter); (2) Leave in place and switch funds within scheme — rejected for Quilter, Nest rejected due to limited fund choice within Nest; (3) Switch to another existing pension arrangement — listed as considered but no rejection reason given; (4) Switch to employer scheme — rejected because client is now self-employed; (5) Set up new pension plan — listed as considered but no rejection reason given. For Nest specifically, the report states the fund is 'not in line with agreed risk level 7' (Nest is level 8) and that while alternative funds might be available within Nest, the new scheme offers wider range.
    File:     [P1] lists the five options considered and states comparisons were made. [P1] states for Quilter: 'Quilter platform charges 0.27%, Aviva 0.19%, saving £208 per annum'; 'You mentioned you had no ties or preference to leave funds with Quilter'; 'ability to actively monitor and adjust investments'. [P2] for True Potential: 'True Potential platform charges 0.40%, Aviva 0.21%, saving 0.19% or £88.68'; 'ongoing fund charges higher in existing 0.58% vs 0.20% in new plan, saving £177.35'; 'portfolio not in line with agreed risk level' and 'While alternative funds within Nest might be available, new scheme offers wider range of investment options'. [P13] describes Nest as 'very simple auto-enrolment scheme designed to provide cheap pension... has few funds available... must be managed by member directly'. Critical yield analysis in [P3], [P4], [P11] shows existing schemes require lower returns than recommended Aviva scheme — Nest at 0.09% vs Aviva at 3.03% (at 2.94% growth). [P3] shows 'The effect moving to Aviva: -12.42%' and 'Reduction in Yield: 1.5%' for True Potential. Death benefit comparison [P11] shows True Potential £46,672 vs Aviva £45,272 (97% of existing benefit). Safeguarded benefits assessment absent from extraction notes.
    Discrepancies:
      - For True Potential and Nest, the report rejects the in-situ switch option citing limited fund choice and broader investment flexibility in Aviva, yet [P13] describes Nest as auto-enrolment with few funds, but no evidence that True Potential fund choice was actually limited or that switching funds within True Potential was impossible or unsuitable.
      - The report states Nest fund 'not in line with agreed risk level' (8 vs 7), justifying switch away, but [P3] and [P4] show critical yield analysis where switching from Nest (which requires only 0.09% growth to match Aviva's projection at 2.94% assumed growth) to Aviva (which requires 3.03% growth) creates a significant outperformance burden — the switch worsens the client's probability of matching existing benefit.
      - For True Potential, report cites 'not in line with agreed risk level' as rationale, but no evidence that True Potential's fund was risk level 6 or 8 — the risk level of the True Potential portfolio is not stated in accessible evidence, only that risk profile is 7.
      - Report lists Option 3 'Switch to another existing pension arrangement' and Option 5 'Set up new pension plan' as considered but provides no rejection reasons or analysis for either option — no evidence they were genuinely evaluated or compared.
      - Death benefit comparison [P11] shows Aviva provides 97% of existing death benefit (£65,907 vs £67,945) — a loss of £2,038 — but the report does not address whether this loss is acceptable or requires remediation through additional insurance or safeguards.
      - Critical yield tables [P3] show at 0% growth existing schemes deliver £48,408 vs Aviva £42,300 (12.42% loss); at 2.94% growth existing deliver £121,807 vs Aviva £106,000 (12.98% loss); this 'day one effect' through charges and fees contradicts the presentation that switching is cost-beneficial.
    The requirement is to assess whether alternatives to the switch were considered and whether the report's conclusions are consistent with and supported by evidence. Five alternatives were considered and documented. However, several discrepancies weaken the justification: (1) For Nest, the critical yield analysis shows switching creates a meaningful outperformance hurdle — the existing Nest fund requires only 0.09% annual growth to match the projected Aviva outcome at 2.94% market growth, whereas Aviva requires 3.03%, a 0.43 percentage point penalty. This contradicts the narrative that switching is justified by risk misalignment; the risk adjustment comes at a material cost measured in basis points of required outperformance. (2) For True Potential, the report cites 'not in line with agreed risk level' but does not establish True Potential's actual risk rating; only the client's agreed profile (7) and recommendation (7) are clear. The charge savings claimed (0.19% platform + 0.38% fund charges = 0.57% total) are offset by the critical yield penalty of 0.43% for True Potential at medium growth, netting approximately 0.14% saving — material but modest. (3) Options 3 and 5 are listed as 'considered' but neither has documented analysis or rejection reasoning; no evidence they were compared. (4) Death benefit shortfall of £2,038 (3%) is calculated but not addressed in recommendations — the report does not disclose this loss or recommend mitigation. (5) The presentation of cost savings centres on platform charges (Quilter 0.27% vs 0.19%, True Potential 0.40% vs 0.21%) but the critical yield analysis shows the all-in charge comparison is less favourable: True Potential's total charges (including fund charges) generate a net cost when added to the critical yield penalty. The guard 'Options assessed once for the whole case may legitimately cover several plans' explains why Options 1–5 are listed once, not per plan; however, it does not excuse the absence of plan-specific evaluation for Options 3 and 5, or the silence on in-situ switching within True Potential. Vulnerability: no vulnerability indicators identified in the evidence that would materially affect this assessment.

  [G9.5] Switching costs exceed the benefits
  Potential Concern — severity High — citations partly verified
    Report:   The report recommends switching three existing arrangements to Aviva. For Quilter (£260,000): platform charges reduce from 0.27% to 0.19%, saving £208 per annum. Active monitoring and a wider fund range are cited. For True Potential (£46,672.80): platform charges reduce from 0.40% to 0.21%, saving 0.19% or £88.68 per annum; a 0.59% reduction is claimed elsewhere. For Nest (£21,272.64): Aviva charges are 0.09% higher at 0.09% more expensive, costing £19.15 more per annum. The report states 'The current fund is not in line with the agreed risk level' (Nest is risk 8, agreed profile is 7). No exit penalties are charged. Out-of-market exposure is 10-15 working days. Benefits cited include active monitoring, wider fund choice, better risk alignment, sophisticated approach, online platform access, and flexible retirement options. Critical Yield tables show day-one transfer effects: Quilter -20.75%, True Potential -14.75% for Nest -31.35% (on True Potential) or -14.75% (on Nest alone). Reduction in Yield figures range from 0.3% to 1.6% depending on scheme and growth scenario. The report asserts these are justified by the non-financial benefits listed.
    File:     The evidence comprises six variations of switching analysis documents (all category G, showing Aviva Pension Portfolio Choice as the receiving scheme). P1 and P5 show True Potential Pension transfer value £46,672.80 with day-one effect -£1,400, Reduction in Yield 1% to 1.1%, and Critical Yields of 0.5% (+0.5%), 3.46% (+0.52%), 6.41% (+0.53%). P2 and P7 show Nest transfer value £21,272.64 with day-one effect -£638, and Critical Yields of 1.19% (+1.19%), 4.17% (+1.23%), 7.14% (+1.26%). P1, P5, P7 show Aviva Annual Management Charge 0.25% pa, reduced by 0.08% (standard charges amended). Fund weightings shown as HSBC 50%, Vanguard 50%, with weighted ongoing costs of 0.2154% (in P2, P4, P6) or adjusted charges. P1 and P5 show True Potential Pension existing value £46,672 with day-one transfer effect -£1,400 to Aviva £45,272.62. P2 and P7 show Nest existing £21,272.64 with day-one transfer effect -£638 to Aviva £20,634.46. No Quilter analysis is present in the evidence. Performance data in P6 shows 12-month weighted average returns for the proposed plan. No documents provide independent comparison of existing vs. proposed arrangements' full cost structure, features, or safeguarded benefits. The evidence does not address whether alternatives to switching were considered or compared.
    Discrepancies:
      - Report asserts Quilter platform charges 0.27% and reduction to 0.19% saving £208 pa; no Quilter switching analysis is present in the evidence.
      - Report states Nest/Aviva charge difference as 0.09% more expensive costing £19.15 pa; evidence shows no standalone Nest switching analysis, only combined analyses that include True Potential or all schemes.
      - Report states True Potential charge reduction of 0.59% (or £275.37 pa 'underperformance'); evidence shows Aviva AMC 0.25% reduced by 0.08% but no explicit True Potential platform charge or cost comparison.
      - Report claims £208 annual saving from Quilter; evidence contains no Quilter transfer value, existing charges, or cost projection.
      - Report cites 'current fund is not in line with the agreed risk level' for Nest (implying risk 8 misalignment); evidence does not confirm Nest fund risk rating or whether alternative lower-risk Nest options were evaluated before recommending switch.
      - Report states day-one effect for Nest transfer as unspecified in the cost section; evidence shows -£638 (P7) or -31.35% effective reduction depending on scenario and scheme combination.
      - Report asserts Critical Yield values of 0.41%, 3.37%, 6.32% on page 32; evidence shows different values: 0.5% (+0.5%), 3.46% (+0.52%), 6.41% (+0.53%) for True Potential and 1.19% (+1.19%), 4.17% (+1.23%), 7.14% (+1.26%) for Nest.
      - Report recommends active monitoring as a benefit; evidence does not corroborate that active monitoring is included or confirm its scope, frequency or cost.
      - Report cites wider fund range as benefit; evidence shows Aviva funds limited to HSBC and Vanguard (2 funds, 50/50 split) with no evidence of a wider range being available or utilised.
      - Report states receiving scheme provides 'more flexible retirement options' and 'tax-efficient withdrawal strategies'; evidence contains no comparison of Nest or True Potential retirement flexibility against Aviva or confirmation of Aviva's flexibility features.
    NOT ASSESSED — the comparison needs a value that was not available: No Quilter (£260,000) switching analysis in evidence; Quilter platform charges 0.27% stated but not corroborated., No standalone cost-benefit analysis document for Nest switch; only combined multi-scheme analyses present., True Potential existing platform charge structure not provided; 0.59% reduction and £275.37 pa claim cannot be verified against independent True Potential cost documentation., No evidence confirming Nest fund risk rating (stated as risk 8 in report) or whether lower-risk alternatives within Nest were considered., No independent evidence of existing arrangements' safeguarded benefits, guarantees or valuable features that would be lost on transfer., No documentation of alternative options considered (e.g., remaining in Nest with lower-risk fund, consolidating to True Potential, or non-advised continuation)., No provider documentation from Quilter, True Potential or Nest confirming charges, fund ranges, or features claimed in the report., No active monitoring service specification or agreement showing scope, frequency and any associated cost., No analysis of whether non-financial benefits (monitoring, wider funds, flexibility) justify the Reduction in Yield for each scheme (ranging 0.3–1.6%)., No personal circumstances sensitivity analysis showing impact of Critical Yield thresholds on client's ability to meet retirement objectives.
    Comparison cannot be reliably performed. The evidence consists of six variations of switching illustrations for Aviva (category G, provider documents), but covers only True Potential and Nest; Quilter analysis is entirely absent. For the two schemes present, the evidence shows switching illustrations but no independent cost analysis or benefit documentation from the existing providers.
    
    For Quilter (£260,000, largest by value): No analysis is present. The report asserts £208 annual savings from a 0.27% to 0.19% charge reduction, but without an independent Quilter cost breakdown or transfer illustration, this cannot be corroborated or contradicted by the evidence.
    
    For True Potential (£46,672.80): Evidence shows Critical Yields of 0.5%, 3.46%, 6.41% (P1) vs. report's stated 0.41%, 3.37%, 6.32%. The differences are small (within 1–3%) but consistent; they may reflect rounding or different assumptions. Evidence shows Reduction in Yield 1% to 1.1%; report does not specify an RIY for True Potential switch. Day-one transfer effect shown as -£1,400 (P1, P5) reducing value from £46,672.80 to £45,272.62. Report claims 0.59% charge reduction and £275.37 pa 'underperformance' but the evidence does not independently confirm True Potential's existing platform charge (0.40% stated in report, not provided in evidence). Aviva AMC is 0.25% reduced by 0.08%, giving net ~0.17%, but this does not clearly show the comparison basis.
    
    For Nest (£21,272.64): Evidence shows Critical Yields of 1.19%, 4.17%, 7.14% (P2) vs. report's stated rates not differentiated per scheme. Day-one transfer effect -£638 (P7). Report asserts Aviva is 0.09% more expensive, costing £19.15 pa on current value; derived figure check confirms £19.15 at 0.09% implies fund value £21,277.78, matching Nest's current value. However, evidence does not show Nest's existing charge structure independently, only the receiving Aviva structure. The report states Nest 'is not in line with the agreed risk level' (risk 8 vs. agreed 7), but evidence does not confirm Nest fund risk rating or show whether a lower-risk fund option within Nest was evaluated.
    
    Non-financial benefits claimed: active monitoring, wider fund range, sophisticated approach, online platform, flexible retirement. Evidence shows only the receiving (Aviva) fund structure (HSBC 50%, Vanguard 50%) and performance data, with no comparison to existing schemes' features or confirmation that active monitoring, wider fund access, or flexibility are included or differentiated vs. existing arrangements.
    
    Alternatives: Report does not disclose whether alternatives such as remaining in Nest with a lower-risk fund, consolidating into True Potential, or staying the course were considered. Evidence provides no alternative comparisons.
    
    Safeguarded benefits/guarantees: Report does not identify any safeguarded benefits, guarantees or valuable features of existing arrangements that would be lost. Evidence does not confirm whether any exist (e.g., Nest minimum charges, employer matching, scheme governance benefits).
    
    Risk alignment issue: Report cites misalignment of Nest (risk 8) with agreed profile (risk 7) as a key reason for switch. Evidence does not corroborate the risk ratings or show that alternatives within Nest were ruled out on documented grounds.
    
    Cost-benefit justification: The report asserts that non-financial benefits justify the Reduction in Yield and Critical Yield requirements, but provides no quantitative or qualitative comparison of existing vs. proposed scheme features. The largest reduction (True Potential showing ~1% RIY, Nest showing 1.5–1.6% RIY) represents material ongoing cost to achieve the claimed benefits, but the evidence does not independently substantiate those benefits' value or that they are unavailable in the existing schemes.
    
    Day-one cash drag: True Potential shows -£1,400 (~3% loss on day 1), Nest shows -£638 (~3% loss on day 1). These are material switching costs on top of ongoing RIY. Report addresses out-of-market exposure (10-15 days) but not the permanent reduction in fund value on transfer day.
    
    Conclusion: Evidence is incomplete for full comparison. Quilter analysis is absent (largest ceding plan). True Potential and Nest switching illustrations are present but lack independent cost documentation from existing providers, independent verification of claimed benefits, or evidence that alternatives were considered. The basis for the comparison (existing charge rates, risk ratings, fund features) cannot be fully verified against supporting documents; the evidence consists only of Aviva receiving scheme illustrations. This constitutes insufficient evidence to support the switching recommendation for any of the three schemes, most critically Quilter.
